Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1003.001
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: process_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"

    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

[LLM] Mailcow Autodiscover endpoint receives unauthenticated XSS payload (GHSA-f9xf-vc72-rcgm) Delivery High
Hunts for unauthenticated POSTs to /Autodiscover/Autodiscover.xml whose EMailAddress field carries HTML/JS tags — the exact unauthenticated XSS-via-logs primitive disclosed in Aikido's Mailcow write-up. These requests are persisted to admin-viewable logs where DataTables renders item.name unescaped. Rationale: Anchors on the article's exact endpoint path (/Autodiscover/Autodiscover.xml) and the HTML/JS tag patterns shown in the PoC payload (<img src=x onerror=alert(origin)>). Defender Advanced Hunting has no native table for nginx/Mailcow access logs so defender_kql is empty; Sentinel Syslog and Splunk Web DM are the realistic targets. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/mailcow/mailcow-dockerized/security/advisories/GHSA-f9xf-vc72-rcgm
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.007
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where SyslogMessage has_cs "Autodiscover"
| where SyslogMessage has "EMailAddress" or SyslogMessage has "/Autodiscover/Autodiscover.xml"
| where SyslogMessage has "<img" or SyslogMessage has "<script" or SyslogMessage has "<svg" or SyslogMessage has "<iframe" or SyslogMessage has "onerror=" or SyslogMessage has "onload=" or SyslogMessage has "javascript:"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostName, Facility, ProcessName, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc
source:nginx @http.method:POST @http.url_details.path:*Autodiscover.xml* ("<img" OR "<script" OR "<svg" OR "<iframe" OR "onerror=" OR "javascript:")
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count from datamodel=Web where Web.http_method=POST AND Web.url="*Autodiscover.xml*" by _time, Web.src, Web.dest, Web.url, Web.http_user_agent, Web.http_referrer
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`

[LLM] Mailcow quarantine XSS via EICAR + HTML in attachment filename (GHSA-2xjc-rg88-jvpp) Delivery High
Detects inbound emails carrying the EICAR antivirus test signature AND an attachment filename containing HTML/JS tags — the exact combination Aikido used to force quarantine + trigger XSS when an admin opens the Quarantine UI. EICAR alone is rare benign noise; EICAR with <img/<script in the filename is essentially never legitimate. Rationale: Anchors on the article's two highly-specific markers: the EICAR test string used to force quarantine and the HTML-tag attachment filename pattern (<img src=x onerror=alert(origin)>.exe). Both together are essentially never legitimate, giving alerting-tier fidelity. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/mailcow/mailcow-dockerized/security/advisories/GHSA-2xjc-rg88-jvpp
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1059.007
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email
EmailAttachmentInfo
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ThreatNames has_any ("EICAR", "EICAR-Test-File", "EICAR_Test_File") or FileName has_cs "EICAR"
| where FileName has "<img" or FileName has "<script" or FileName has "<svg" or FileName has "<iframe" or FileName has "onerror=" or FileName has "onload="
| join kind=leftouter (EmailEvents | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderIPv4, SenderIPv6, EmailDirection, DeliveryAction) on NetworkMessageId
| project Timestamp, NetworkMessageId, SenderFromAddress, RecipientEmailAddress, Subject, FileName, FileType, ThreatNames, MalwareFilterVerdict, SenderIPv4, EmailDirection, DeliveryAction
| order by Timestamp desc
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ProcessName has_any ("rspamd", "clamd", "clamav", "amavis", "postfix", "mailcow") or Facility =~ "mail"
| where SyslogMessage has_cs "EICAR"
| where SyslogMessage has "filename="
| where SyslogMessage has "<img" or SyslogMessage has "<script" or SyslogMessage has "<svg" or SyslogMessage has "<iframe" or SyslogMessage has "onerror=" or SyslogMessage has "onload="
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostName, Facility, ProcessName, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Mailcow login with HTML/JS injected into X-Real-IP header (GHSA-jprq-w83q-q62h) Delivery Medium
Hunts for HTTP requests to Mailcow's login / user-area endpoints where the X-Real-IP header contains HTML or JavaScript syntax. This is the Login-CSRF + stored Self-XSS chain Aikido described: the attacker forges X-Real-IP on their own login so the payload lands in the real_rip column rendered unescaped on the admin's Login History page after a forced login. Rationale: X-Real-IP is meant to carry an IPv4/IPv6 string only; any occurrence of HTML tags inside that header value or in Mailcow's real_rip column is by definition exploitation of GHSA-jprq-w83q-q62h since the article confirms the field is trusted and unvalidated. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/mailcow/mailcow-dockerized/security/advisories/GHSA-jprq-w83q-q62h
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.007 T1539
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where Computer has "mailcow" or ProcessName has_any ("nginx", "mailcow", "sogo", "dovecot")
| where SyslogMessage has "X-Real-IP" or SyslogMessage has "real_rip"
| where SyslogMessage has "<img" or SyslogMessage has "<script" or SyslogMessage has "<svg" or SyslogMessage has "<iframe" or SyslogMessage has "onerror=" or SyslogMessage has "onload=" or SyslogMessage has "javascript:"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostName, Facility, ProcessName, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc
source:(nginx OR mailcow) ("X-Real-IP" OR "real_rip") ("<img" OR "<script" OR "<svg" OR "<iframe" OR "onerror=" OR "onload=")

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine

// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)

CISA KEV: CVE-2026-34197 — Apache ActiveMQ Improper Input Validation Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-04-16 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Apache ActiveMQ contains an improper input validation vulnerability that allows for code injection. Vendor: Apache, Product: ActiveMQ. Federal patch due: 2026-04-30.

Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-34197")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-34197")

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1003.001
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: process_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

[LLM] Trust Wallet Shai-Hulud C2 callback to metrics-trustwallet.com / 138.124.70.40 Command & Control High
Detects network or DNS activity to the attacker-controlled exfil/C2 infrastructure used by the trojanized Trust Wallet browser extension v2.68 and the Shai-Hulud 2.0 worm. The domain pair was staged on Stark Industries (AS44477) on 2025-12-08 and used to harvest wallet mnemonics and developer secrets from compromised endpoints. Rationale: Domain pair metrics-trustwallet.com / api.metrics-trustwallet.com and resolving IP 138.124.70.40 (Stark Industries AS44477) are confirmed across Trust Wallet's incident update, The Hacker News, Halborn, BleepingComputer and Cybernews writeups as exclusive attacker infrastructure with no legitimate use. Hash-grade fidelity. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2025/12/trust-wallet-chrome-extension-hack.html • https://trustwallet.com/blog/announcements/trust-wallet-browser-extension-v268-incident-community-update • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/trust-wallet-links-85-million-crypto-theft-to-shai-hulud-npm-attack/ • https://www.halborn.com/blog/post/explained-the-trust-wallet-hack-december-2025 • https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2025/12/09/shai-hulud-2-0-guidance-for-detecting-investigating-and-defending-against-the-supply-chain-attack/

let badIPs = dynamic(["138.124.70.40"]);

| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has_any (badDomains) or RemoteIP in (badIPs)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl, ActionType
| order by Timestamp desc
let badDomains = dynamic(["metrics-trustwallet.com","api.metrics-trustwallet.com"]);
let badIPs = dynamic(["138.124.70.40"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  (ImDnsActivity
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where DnsQuery has_any (badDomains)
   | project TimeGenerated, SrcHostname, SrcIpAddr, SrcUsername, DnsQuery, DnsResponseName, EventProduct),
  (ImNetworkSession
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where DstIpAddr in (badIPs) or DstHostname has_any (badDomains)
   | project TimeGenerated, SrcHostname, SrcIpAddr, SrcUsername, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, EventProduct),
  (CommonSecurityLog
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where DestinationHostName has_any (badDomains) or DestinationIP in (badIPs) or RequestURL has_any (badDomains)
   | project TimeGenerated, SourceHostName, SourceIP, DestinationHostName, DestinationIP, RequestURL, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

author: SOC Detection Engineering

  category: dns
detection:

    query|contains:
      - 'metrics-trustwallet.com'

  - Threat-intel researchers retrieving samples
  - EDR sandbox replay of captured malicious extension
level: high
(source:(windows.sysmon OR windows.defender) AND @EventID:(22 OR 3) AND (@QueryName:(metrics-trustwallet.com OR api.metrics-trustwallet.com) OR @DestinationHostname:(metrics-trustwallet.com OR api.metrics-trustwallet.com) OR @DestinationIp:138.124.70.40)) OR (source:cloudtrail AND @network.client.ip:138.124.70.40)
(#event_simpleName=DnsRequest DomainName=/^(api\.)?metrics-trustwallet\.com$/i) OR (#event_simpleName=/^NetworkConnectIP[46]$/ RemoteAddressIP4="138.124.70.40")
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, DomainName, RemoteAddressIP4], function=[count(), min(@timestamp, as=firstSeen), max(@timestamp, as=lastSeen)])

[LLM] Shai-Hulud 2.0 npm worm artifact: setup_bun.js / bun_environment.js dropped by node/npm Installation High
Detects the two malicious payload files written to disk by the Shai-Hulud 2.0 npm worm via a poisoned preinstall script. bun_environment.js is a ~10MB heavily-obfuscated harvester; setup_bun.js is the bootstrap. Both are written by node.exe / npm under a developer's home or repo path during package install. Rationale: Files setup_bun.js and bun_environment.js are documented across Datadog Security Labs, Microsoft, Unit 42, Sysdig, Netskope, Wiz and Trend Micro as the canonical Shai-Hulud 2.0 worm artifacts dropped by the malicious npm preinstall script. The ~10MB obfuscated bun_environment.js is unique to this campaign - filename + node/npm parent is hash-grade. Cross-checked against: • https://securitylabs.datadoghq.com/articles/shai-hulud-2.0-npm-worm/ • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/npm-supply-chain-attack/ • https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2025/12/09/shai-hulud-2-0-guidance-for-detecting-investigating-and-defending-against-the-supply-chain-attack/ • https://www.sysdig.com/blog/return-of-the-shai-hulud-worm-affects-over-25-000-github-repositories • https://github.com/DataDog/indicators-of-compromise/tree/main/shai-hulud-2.0
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007 T1546.016
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where FileName in~ ("setup_bun.js","bun_environment.js")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","npm.cmd","npm.exe","npx.cmd","yarn.exe","yarn.cmd","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","bun.exe","sh.exe","bash.exe")
   or FolderPath has_any (@"\node_modules\",@"\.npm\",@"\AppData\Local\npm\",@"\AppData\Roaming\npm\")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, FileSize
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("setup_bun.js","bun_environment.js")
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("node.exe","npm.cmd","npm.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","bun.exe","sh","bash")
   or TargetFilePath has_any ("\\node_modules\\","/node_modules/","\\.npm\\","/.npm/")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSize, TargetFileSHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc

      - '\sh.exe'
      - '\bash.exe'
  condition: selection_file and selection_writer
falsepositives:
  - Developer manually authoring a file literally named bun_environment.js (rare)
  - Bun runtime installer (writes to %LOCALAPPDATA%\bun, not into node_modules)
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 @TargetFilename:(*\\setup_bun.js OR *\\bun_environment.js OR */setup_bun.js OR */bun_environment.js) @Image:(*\\node.exe OR *\\npm.cmd OR *\\npm.exe OR *\\yarn.exe OR *\\pnpm.exe OR *\\bun.exe OR *\\sh.exe OR *\\bash.exe OR *\\NODE.EXE OR *\\NPM.EXE)
#event_simpleName=FileWritten TargetFileName=/(\\|\/)(setup_bun|bun_environment)\.js$/i
| ContextImageFileName=/(\\|\/)(node|npm|npx|yarn|pnpm|bun|sh|bash)(\.exe|\.cmd)?$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ContextImageFileName, TargetFileName], function=[count(), min(@timestamp, as=firstWrite), max(WrittenBytes, as=maxBytes)])
ENDPOINT

[LLM] Shai-Hulud preinstall: node/npm spawning git/curl/gh pushing to attacker repo or GitHub API Actions on Objectives Medium
Hunts for the Shai-Hulud 2.0 self-propagation/exfil step where the dropped bun_environment.js forks a detached node process that uses git, gh, or curl to create a public GitHub repo (name matching [0-9a-z]{18}) tagged 'Sha1-Hulud: The Second Coming' and push the harvested credentials. Pivots on the parent=node.exe/npm child=git/curl/gh chain that's atypical of normal install flows. Rationale: Self-propagation step is documented by Datadog Security Labs and Microsoft - the worm uses victim npm/GitHub tokens to create [0-9a-z]{18}-named public repos with description 'Sha1-Hulud: The Second Coming' and pushes harvested credentials. Pivot on the unusual parent=node child=git/gh/curl chain combined with credential-file path tokens in cmdline. Cross-checked against: • https://securitylabs.datadoghq.com/articles/shai-hulud-2.0-npm-worm/ • https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2025/12/09/shai-hulud-2-0-guidance-for-detecting-investigating-and-defending-against-the-supply-chain-attack/ • https://www.netskope.com/blog/shai-hulud-2-0-aggressive-automated-one-of-fastest-spreading-npm-supply-chain-attacks-ever-observed • https://www.wiz.io/blog/shai-hulud-2-0-ongoing-supply-chain-attack
ATT&CKT1567 T1059.007 T1078.004 T1552.001

description: |

        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'

Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — @velora-dex/sdk Compromised on npm: Malicious Version Drops macOS Backdoor via l
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] launchctl persistence registering zsh.profiler service from non-admin location · [LLM] macOS file write of profiler binary to com.apple.Terminal masquerade path
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Outbound connection to Velora DEX npm supply-chain C2 89.36.224.5 · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] Outbound connection to Velora DEX npm supply-chain C2 89.36.224.5 Command & Control High
Detect any host beaconing to the @velora-dex/sdk@9.4.1 dropper IP 89.36.224.5 over plaintext HTTP. The address serves install.sh and the architecture-specific profiler binaries; any direct contact from a developer workstation, build agent or CI runner is a compromise indicator. Rationale: 89.36.224.5 is the unique C2 address explicitly named in the StepSecurity write-up (table: 'Network Events: C2 Contact Confirmed' and the three IOC URLs at the end). Cross-checked against the original Aikido GitHub issue #233 reference and the article body. A single IP IOC with no legitimate business reason on any endpoint makes this a very high-fidelity alert. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/velora-dex-sdk-compromised-on-npm-malicious-version-drops-macos-backdoor-via-launchctl-persistence • https://github.com/paraswap/paraswap-sdk/issues/233
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1105
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteIP == "89.36.224.5"
   or RemoteUrl has_any ("89.36.224.5/troubleshoot/mac/install.sh","89.36.224.5/mac/arm/driver/profiler","89.36.224.5/mac/intel/driver/profiler")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DestinationIP == "89.36.224.5" or RequestURL has "89.36.224.5"
    | project TimeGenerated, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, SourceIP, SourceUserName, DestinationIP, DestinationPort, RequestURL, DeviceAction),
  (ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DstIpAddr == "89.36.224.5"
    | project TimeGenerated, SrcHostname, SrcIpAddr, SrcUsername, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkApplicationProtocol),
  (DnsEvents
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where RemoteIp == "89.36.224.5" or Name has "89.36.224.5"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, ClientIP, Name, QueryType, RemoteIp)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] node process spawning bash/curl chain to fetch Velora DEX install.sh dropper Delivery High
Detects the require()-time payload that prepends three lines to dist/index.js of @velora-dex/sdk@9.4.1 — node spawns /bin/sh -> base64 -> bash -> curl http://89.36.224.5/troubleshoot/mac/install.sh. The base64 blob and the install.sh URL are both unique fingerprints. Rationale: Anchored on two extremely unique strings from the article body: the exact base64 blob prepended to dist/index.js and the exact URL it decodes to. Both strings have no legitimate use anywhere on Earth — a single hit is a confirmed compromise. Also catches the post-decode bash + curl execution branch. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/velora-dex-sdk-compromised-on-npm-malicious-version-drops-macos-backdoor-via-launchctl-persistence

[LLM] launchctl persistence registering zsh.profiler service from non-admin location Installation High
Detects the persistence step of the @velora-dex/sdk@9.4.1 backdoor — `launchctl submit -l zsh.profiler -- $HOME/Library/Application Support/com.apple.Terminal/profiler`. The unique label `zsh.profiler` and the masquerade path are both article-specific fingerprints that survive across reboots. Rationale: Verbatim from the article's install.sh body: `launchctl submit -l zsh.profiler -- "$PROFILER_PATH"` where $PROFILER_PATH=$HOME/Library/Application Support/com.apple.Terminal/profiler. The label string `zsh.profiler` does not appear in any Apple-supplied launchd plist. The IOC list at the end of the article re-confirms both the service label and the filesystem path. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/velora-dex-sdk-compromised-on-npm-malicious-version-drops-macos-backdoor-via-launchctl-persistence
ATT&CKT1543.004 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName =~ "launchctl"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "submit"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("zsh.profiler","com.apple.Terminal/profiler","Library/Application Support/com.apple.Terminal/profiler")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine, ProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
  (ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where ActingProcessName has "launchctl" or TargetProcessName has "launchctl"
    | where TargetProcessCommandLine has "submit"
    | where TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("zsh.profiler","com.apple.Terminal/profiler")
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine),
  (Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where ProcessName has "launchctl"
    | where SyslogMessage has_any ("zsh.profiler","com.apple.Terminal/profiler")
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, ProcessName, SyslogMessage)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] macOS file write of profiler binary to com.apple.Terminal masquerade path Installation High
Detects the architecture-specific Mach-O binary drop at ~/Library/Application Support/com.apple.Terminal/profiler from the @velora-dex/sdk@9.4.1 install.sh dropper. The path mimics a legitimate Terminal.app support directory and the writing process is curl or bash spawned by node — a high-fidelity combination. Rationale: The path string `~/Library/Application Support/com.apple.Terminal/profiler` and the file name `profiler` are both explicitly listed in the IOC block at the end of the article and visible in the install.sh body. Apple's actual Terminal.app does not create a `profiler` file in that directory — any write there is high-confidence malicious. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/velora-dex-sdk-compromised-on-npm-malicious-version-drops-macos-backdoor-via-launchctl-persistence

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
Network connections to article IPs / domains Command & Control High
Outbound traffic to attacker infrastructure named in the article.
ATT&CKT1071
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web Network_Resolution.DNS DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteIP in ("89.36.224.5") or RemoteUrl has_any ("")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
let BadIPs     = dynamic(["89.36.224.5"]);
let BadDomains = dynamic([""]);
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Behind the Scenes: How StepSecurity Detected and Helped Remediate the Largest npm Supply Chain Attack

2026-04-09 8 use cases 3 techniques 7 kill-chain phases detected

TeamPCP weaponized 76 Trivy version tags overnight. The KICS attack followed the same playbook days later. One security control is not enough. Here is how the StepSecurity platform's ten independent security layers work together to prevent credential exfiltration, detect compromised actions at runtime, and respond to incidents across your entire organization before attackers can succeed.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] TeamPCP sysmon.py systemd-user persistence on developer host · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] TeamPCP Trivy/KICS C2 callback to scan.aquasecurtiy.org / 45.148.10.212 · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Read of /proc/<pid>/mem targeting GitHub Runner.Worker (TeamPCP credential dump)
[LLM] TeamPCP Trivy/KICS C2 callback to scan.aquasecurtiy.org / 45.148.10.212 Command & Control High
Hunts for any network egress (DNS resolution, HTTP POST, raw TCP) to the typosquat C2 used by the compromised aquasecurity/trivy-action (v0.0.1 through v0.34.2) and Checkmarx KICS GitHub Actions, attributed to threat group TeamPCP (CVE-2026-33634). A single hit on this domain or IP from a GitHub Actions self-hosted/ARC runner or developer host is a near-certain compromise indicator because the domain is a typosquat with no legitimate use case. Rationale: Domain scan.aquasecurtiy.org (typosquat — note the swapped 'urtiy' vs legitimate 'urity') and IP 45.148.10.212 are explicit IOCs in the StepSecurity article and cross-confirmed by Wiz, Aqua advisory GHSA-69fq-xp46-6x23, Microsoft Security blog, CrowdStrike, and Snyk write-ups of the same TeamPCP campaign. No legitimate workflow ever resolves this typosquat, so a single hit is high-fidelity. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy/security/advisories/GHSA-69fq-xp46-6x23 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/trivy-compromised-teampcp-supply-chain-attack • https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2026/03/24/detecting-investigating-defending-against-trivy-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://www.crowdstrike.com/en-us/blog/from-scanner-to-stealer-inside-the-trivy-action-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://www.aquasec.com/blog/trivy-supply-chain-attack-what-you-need-to-know/

[LLM] Read of /proc/<pid>/mem targeting GitHub Runner.Worker (TeamPCP credential dump) Actions on Objectives High
Hunts for a process opening /proc/<pid>/mem on a Linux GitHub Actions runner — the exact behaviour the trivy-action and KICS payloads used to scrape secrets from the Runner.Worker process before exfiltrating to TeamPCP C2. /proc/<pid>/mem cross-process reads have no legitimate use case in standard CI/CD pipelines, and the article specifically names this as the unique runtime signal. Rationale: The StepSecurity article explicitly names the unique runtime signal: "the malware spawned a Python process that read /proc/pid/mem of the GitHub Actions Runner.Worker process to extract marked secrets from memory." Cross-process /proc/<pid>/mem reads outside debugger tooling are virtually unheard of in normal CI/CD workloads — corroborated by Wiz, Aqua advisory GHSA-69fq-xp46-6x23, and Microsoft Defender guidance on the same campaign which all flag this as the canonical TTP. Path regex anchors `/proc/<numeric pid>/mem` to avoid matching unrelated /proc paths. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy/security/advisories/GHSA-69fq-xp46-6x23 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/trivy-compromised-teampcp-supply-chain-attack • https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2026/03/24/detecting-investigating-defending-against-trivy-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://www.crowdstrike.com/en-us/blog/from-scanner-to-stealer-inside-the-trivy-action-supply-chain-compromise/
ATT&CKT1003.007 T1552.001 T1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes
// Defender for Endpoint on Linux — read of /proc/<pid>/mem
let legit_debuggers = dynamic(["gdb","strace","ltrace","crash","perf","makedumpfile","criu","systemd-coredump"]);
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where DeviceName !startswith "WIN"
| where (FolderPath matches regex @"^/proc/[0-9]+$" and FileName =~ "mem")
     or (FolderPath matches regex @"^/proc/[0-9]+/mem$")
     or (FileName =~ "mem" and FolderPath has "/proc/")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ (legit_debuggers)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, DeviceId, FolderPath, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName,
          InitiatingProcessAccountName
| join kind=leftouter (
    DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("Runner.Worker","/proc/","trivy-action","aquasecurity","kics")
    | project DeviceId, CtxTime=Timestamp, CtxCmd=ProcessCommandLine, CtxFile=FileName
  ) on DeviceId
| order by Timestamp desc
// ASIM file events — read of /proc/<pid>/mem on Linux runners
let legit_debuggers = dynamic(["gdb","strace","ltrace","crash","perf","makedumpfile","criu","systemd-coredump"]);
union isfuzzy=true
( ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where TargetFilePath matches regex @"^/proc/[0-9]+/mem$"
    | where ActingProcessName !in~ (legit_debuggers)
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFilePath,
              ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, EventType ),
( Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Facility in~ ("auth","authpriv","daemon","kern","user")
    | where SyslogMessage matches regex @"/proc/[0-9]+/mem"
    | where SyslogMessage !has "gdb" and SyslogMessage !has "strace"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostIP, ProcessName, SyslogMessage )
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Cross-Process Read of /proc/<pid>/mem (TeamPCP Runner.Worker Credential Dump)
id: 8e2b9f4c-6a31-4c87-bf12-3e7a91d4c2b0
description: Detects a process opening /proc/<pid>/mem of another process on Linux. Used by the TeamPCP-compromised aquasecurity/trivy-action and Checkmarx KICS GitHub Actions (CVE-2026-33634) to dump secrets from the Runner.Worker process.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/behind-the-scenes-how-stepsecurity-detected-and-helped-remediate-the-largest-npm-supply-chain-attack
  - https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy/security/advisories/GHSA-69fq-xp46-6x23
  - https://www.wiz.io/blog/trivy-compromised-teampcp-supply-chain-attack
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/13
tags:
  - attack.t1003.007
  - attack.t1552.001
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.credential_access
logsource:
  product: linux
  category: file_access
detection:
  selection:
    TargetFilename|re: '^/proc/[0-9]+/mem$'
  filter_legit:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/gdb'
      - '/strace'
      - '/ltrace'
      - '/crash'
      - '/perf'
      - '/makedumpfile'
      - '/criu'
      - '/systemd-coredump'
  condition: selection and not filter_legit
falsepositives:
  - Authorised debuggers/profilers (gdb, strace, perf) — filtered above.
  - Container-runtime introspection tools; tune per environment.
level: high
source:linux.auditd AND (@auditd.path:/proc/*/mem OR @file.path:/proc/*/mem OR @process.command_line:(*"/proc/"*"/mem"*)) AND -@process.executable.path:(/usr/bin/gdb OR /usr/bin/strace OR /usr/bin/ltrace OR /usr/bin/perf OR /usr/bin/crash OR /usr/bin/makedumpfile OR /usr/sbin/criu OR /lib/systemd/systemd-coredump)

[LLM] TeamPCP sysmon.py systemd-user persistence on developer host Installation High
Hunts for creation of the persistence dropper sysmon.py under ~/.config/systemd/user/ — the post-execution artefact left on developer machines that ran a malicious local Trivy binary as part of the TeamPCP campaign (CVE-2026-33634). The filename and path are explicit IOCs published in the follow-up StepSecurity / Aqua advisories and are unique to this campaign. Rationale: Article and cross-vendor follow-ups specifically name the post-execution persistence dropper at ~/.config/systemd/user/sysmon.py installed by the TeamPCP trojanised Trivy binary on developer hosts. The path+filename combination is unique to this campaign — no benign software writes sysmon.py to a per-user systemd unit directory — making this a near-zero-FP file-create signal. Corroborated in the StepSecurity follow-up (second compromise post), Aqua advisory, and Halcyon/Phoenix Security write-ups. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/trivy-compromised-a-second-time---malicious-v0-69-4-release • https://www.aquasec.com/blog/trivy-supply-chain-attack-what-you-need-to-know/ • https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy/security/advisories/GHSA-69fq-xp46-6x23 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/trivy-compromised-teampcp-supply-chain-attack
ATT&CKT1543.002 T1546 T1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
// Creation of sysmon.py under ~/.config/systemd/user/ (TeamPCP persistence)
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where FolderPath has @"/.config/systemd/user"
| where FileName =~ "sysmon.py"
   or (FileName endswith ".service" and FileName has "sysmon")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, DeviceId, ActionType, FolderPath, FileName, SHA256,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| join kind=leftouter (
    DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("trivy","aquasecurity","setup-trivy")
    | summarize TrivyExecCount=count(), LastTrivyExec=max(Timestamp) by DeviceId
  ) on DeviceId
| order by Timestamp desc
// ASIM ImFileEvent — sysmon.py systemd-user persistence dropper
union isfuzzy=true
( ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where EventType in~ ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
    | where TargetFilePath has "/.config/systemd/user"
    | where TargetFileName =~ "sysmon.py"
       or (TargetFileName endswith ".service" and TargetFileName has "sysmon")
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
              ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName,
              TargetFileSHA256 ),
( Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where SyslogMessage has "/.config/systemd/user/sysmon.py"
       or (SyslogMessage has "systemd" and SyslogMessage has "sysmon.py")
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostIP, ProcessName, SyslogMessage )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic

    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic

ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-33634")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-33634")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["887e1f5b5b50162a60bd03b66269e0ae545d0aef0583c1c5b00972152ad7e073", "f7084b0229dce605ccc5506b14acd4d954a496da4b6134a294844ca8d601970d", "822dd269ec10459572dfaaefe163dae693c344249a0161953f0d5cdd110bd2a0", "bef7e2c5a92c4fa4af17791efc1e46311c0f304796f1172fce192f5efc40f5d7", "e64e152afe2c722d750f10259626f357cdea40420c5eedab37969fbf13abbecf", "ecce7ae5ffc9f57bb70efd3ea136a2923f701334a8cd47d4fbf01a97fd22859c", "d5edd791021b966fb6af0ace09319ace7b97d6642363ef27b3d5056ca654a94c", "e6310d8a003d7ac101a6b1cd39ff6c6a88ee454b767c1bdce143e04bc1111343", "6328a34b26a63423b555a61f89a6a0525a534e9c88584c815d937910f1ddd538", "0880819ef821cff918960a39c1c1aada55a5593c61c608ea9215da858a86e349"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

axios Compromised on npm - Malicious Versions Drop Remote Access Trojan

StepSecurity
2026-04-09 12 use cases 7 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

Hijacked maintainer account used to publish poisoned axios releases including 1.14.1 and 0.30.4. The attacker injected a hidden dependency that drops a cross platform RAT. We are actively investigating and will update this post with a full technical analysis.

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Malicious axios or plain-crypto-js package files written to node_modules
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — axios Compromised on npm - Malicious Versions Drop Remote Access Trojan
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] npm postinstall node setup.js dropper executing from plain-crypto-js with immediate network egress · RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] axios Supply Chain RAT C2 Callback to sfrclak.com (Port 8000) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] plain-crypto-js setup.js self-deletion or package.json overwrite (anti-forensics) · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
[LLM] axios Supply Chain RAT C2 Callback to sfrclak.com (Port 8000) Command & Control High
Detects DNS resolution or outbound network egress to the hard-coded C2 domain sfrclak.com used by the malicious plain-crypto-js@4.2.1 dropper injected by hijacked axios@1.14.1 and axios@0.30.4 packages. StepSecurity Harden-Runner publicly flagged this exact callback during a Backstage CI run, confirming the IOC is high-fidelity for any host that installed the poisoned versions. Rationale: sfrclak.com is the article's named C2 and the registered Harden-Runner anomaly URL (Backstage run 23775668703) corroborates it as the real callback. Port 8000 is article-stated. A single connection to either is detection-grade — domain has no benign use. Cross-checked against: • https://app.stepsecurity.io/github/backstage/backstage/actions/runs/23775668703?tab=network-events
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1568
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
union
  (DeviceNetworkEvents
   | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
   | where RemoteUrl has "sfrclak.com" or (tolower(RemoteUrl) endswith "sfrclak.com" and RemotePort == 8000)
   | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort, Evidence="network_connect"),
  (DeviceEvents
   | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
   | where ActionType in ("DnsQueryResponse","DnsConnectionInspected")
   | where AdditionalFields has "sfrclak.com" or RemoteUrl has "sfrclak.com"
   | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, Evidence="dns_query")
| order by Timestamp desc
let target = "sfrclak.com";
union isfuzzy=true
  (DnsEvents
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where Name endswith target
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, ClientIP, Name, QueryType, ResultCodeName, Evidence="dns_query"),
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DestinationHostName endswith target or RequestURL has target or (DestinationPort == 8000 and isnotempty(DestinationHostName))
    | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, SourceHostName, DestinationIP, DestinationHostName, DestinationPort, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, RequestURL, Evidence="perimeter_egress"),
  (ImDnsActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DnsQuery endswith target
    | project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, SrcHostname, DnsQuery, DnsResponseCodeName, EventProduct, Evidence="asim_dns"),
  (ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DstHostname endswith target or (DstPortNumber == 8000 and DstHostname endswith target)
    | project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, SrcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, DstPortNumber, EventProduct, Evidence="asim_netflow")
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: axios Supply Chain RAT C2 Callback to sfrclak.com
id: 6f1e8d2a-3c4b-4a8f-9e1d-2b5c3a8e7d4f
status: experimental
description: Detects DNS queries to sfrclak.com - the C2 domain used by the malicious plain-crypto-js@4.2.1 RAT dropper injected as a fake dependency by hijacked axios@1.14.1 and axios@0.30.4 npm packages.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/axios-compromised-on-npm-malicious-versions-drop-remote-access-trojan
  - https://app.stepsecurity.io/github/backstage/backstage/actions/runs/23775668703?tab=network-events
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/03/30
tags:
  - attack.command_and_control
  - attack.t1071.001
  - attack.t1568
logsource:
  category: dns
detection:
  selection:
    QueryName|endswith: 'sfrclak.com'
  condition: selection
falsepositives:
  - None expected; sfrclak.com has no legitimate use.
level: high
(source:windows.sysmon AND ((@EventID:22 AND @QueryName:*sfrclak.com) OR (@EventID:3 AND (@DestinationHostname:*sfrclak.com OR (@DestinationPort:8000 AND @DestinationHostname:*sfrclak.com))))) OR (source:linux.syslog AND @network.destination.name:*sfrclak.com) OR (source:linux.auditd AND @network.destination.name:*sfrclak.com)
(#event_simpleName=DnsRequest DomainName=/sfrclak\.com$/i)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, DomainName, ContextImageFileName, ContextProcessId], function=count(as=hits))
| join({#event_simpleName=NetworkConnectIP4 RemotePort=8000}, field=aid, include=[RemoteAddressIP4, RemotePort, ContextImageFileName], mode=left)

[LLM] npm postinstall node setup.js dropper executing from plain-crypto-js with immediate network egress Installation High
Detects the exact execution chain the axios supply-chain RAT dropper uses: npm install spawns node.exe to run `node setup.js` from a `plain-crypto-js` package directory, then makes an outbound network connection within ~2 seconds (per the article, callback occurred before npm even finished resolving dependencies). This is the article-specific behavioural shape — a postinstall script in a fake transitive dependency reaching the internet during install. Rationale: The article specifies the exact malicious chain: postinstall hook is `node setup.js` inside the `plain-crypto-js` package, and callback fires within ~2s of `npm install` before dependency resolution completes. Combining the parent=npm/node, child=node, cmdline=plain-crypto-js/setup.js, plus near-immediate network egress yields a near-zero-FP signature that no benign postinstall would match. Cross-checked against: • https://app.stepsecurity.io/github/backstage/backstage/actions/runs/23775668703?tab=network-events
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007 T1546.016
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
let SetupRun = DeviceProcessEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
  | where FileName in~ ("node.exe","node")
  | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("npm.exe","node.exe","npm","npm-cli.js","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm","yarn")
     or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("npm install","npm i ","npm ci","yarn install","pnpm install")
  | where ProcessCommandLine has "setup.js"
  | where ProcessCommandLine has "plain-crypto-js" or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has "plain-crypto-js" or FolderPath has "plain-crypto-js"
  | project SetupTime=Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessId, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName;
SetupRun
| join kind=inner (
    DeviceNetworkEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","node")
    | where RemoteIPType == "Public"
    | project NetTime=Timestamp, DeviceId, ChildProcId=InitiatingProcessId, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort
  ) on DeviceId
| where ChildProcId == ProcessId
| where datetime_diff('second', NetTime, SetupTime) between (0 .. 60)
| project SetupTime, NetTime, DelaySec=datetime_diff('second', NetTime, SetupTime), DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort
| order by SetupTime desc
let WindowSec = 60;
let SetupRun = ImProcessCreate
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where ActingProcessName has_any ("node.exe","/node")
  | where ParentProcessName has_any ("npm","node","yarn","pnpm")
     or ParentProcessCommandLine has_any ("npm install","npm i ","npm ci","yarn install","pnpm install","postinstall")
  | where ActingProcessCommandLine has "setup.js"
  | where ActingProcessCommandLine has "plain-crypto-js" or ParentProcessCommandLine has "plain-crypto-js"
  | project SetupTime=TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessId, ActingProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessCommandLine;
SetupRun
| join kind=inner (
    ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where SrcHostname != ""
    | project NetTime=TimeGenerated, DvcHostname=SrcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, DstPortNumber
  ) on DvcHostname
| where datetime_diff('second', NetTime, SetupTime) between (0 .. WindowSec)
| project SetupTime, NetTime, DelaySec=datetime_diff('second', NetTime, SetupTime), DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessCommandLine, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, DstPortNumber
| order by SetupTime desc

[LLM] Malicious axios or plain-crypto-js package files written to node_modules Delivery High
Hunts for the on-disk artifacts of the axios supply-chain compromise: `node_modules/plain-crypto-js/setup.js` (the 4.2 KB obfuscated dropper added in 4.2.1), `node_modules/plain-crypto-js/package.md` (the anti-forensics decoy), and `node_modules/axios` directories whose `package.json` reports version `1.14.1` or `0.30.4`. Catches infected hosts even after the dropper self-deletes if file-event telemetry is retained. Rationale: Article-stated artifacts: setup.js is the 4.2 KB dropper added in plain-crypto-js@4.2.1 (Table 2: 'Added: the RAT dropper'), package.md is the anti-forensics cover stub (Table 2: 'Added: the anti-forensics cover'). Both file names within node_modules/plain-crypto-js/ have no legitimate purpose - the legitimate 4.2.0 release had neither file.
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where (FolderPath has @"\node_modules\plain-crypto-js" or FolderPath has "/node_modules/plain-crypto-js")
   and FileName in~ ("setup.js","package.md","package.json")
| extend IsDropper = (FileName =~ "setup.js")
| extend IsAntiForensic = (FileName =~ "package.md")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath, FileName, SHA256, FileSize, IsDropper, IsAntiForensic
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in~ ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed","FileCreate")
| where (TargetFilePath has @"\node_modules\plain-crypto-js" or TargetFilePath has "/node_modules/plain-crypto-js")
   and TargetFileName in~ ("setup.js","package.md","package.json")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSize, TargetFileSHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] plain-crypto-js setup.js self-deletion or package.json overwrite (anti-forensics) Actions on Objectives Medium
Detects the article's distinctive anti-forensics behaviour: after first-run, the RAT dropper deletes its own setup.js and overwrites package.json with a clean decoy reporting version 4.2.0, so a developer auditing node_modules later sees nothing unusual. The deletion + same-directory package.json rewrite by node.exe is a high-fidelity tamper signal. Rationale: Article explicitly describes the anti-forensics: setup.js self-deletes and package.json is overwritten with a clean stub reporting version 4.2.0 (Table 2: package.md added as cover; narrative: 'erases itself and replaces its own package.json with a clean decoy'). Pairing the deletion of setup.js with a near-simultaneous package.json rewrite by node.exe in the same plain-crypto-js directory is a unique behavioural fingerprint.
ATT&CKT1070.004 T1564.001 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
let TargetDir = "plain-crypto-js";
let Deletions = DeviceFileEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where ActionType in ("FileDeleted","FileRenamed")
  | where FolderPath has TargetDir
  | where FileName =~ "setup.js" or PreviousFileName =~ "setup.js"
  | project DelTime=Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, DelActor=InitiatingProcessFileName, DelActorCmd=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, DelPath=FolderPath;
let Rewrites = DeviceFileEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where ActionType in ("FileModified","FileCreated")
  | where FolderPath has TargetDir and FileName =~ "package.json"
  | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","node")
  | project RewriteTime=Timestamp, DeviceId, RewriteActor=InitiatingProcessFileName, RewriteCmd=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RewriteSHA=SHA256;
Deletions
| join kind=inner Rewrites on DeviceId
| where abs(datetime_diff('second', RewriteTime, DelTime)) <= 30
| project DelTime, RewriteTime, DeviceName, AccountName, DelActor, DelActorCmd, DelPath, RewriteActor, RewriteCmd, RewriteSHA
| order by DelTime desc
let TargetDir = "plain-crypto-js";
let Deletions = ImFileEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where EventType in~ ("FileDeleted","FileRenamed","FileDelete")
  | where TargetFilePath has TargetDir and (TargetFileName =~ "setup.js")
  | project DelTime=TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, DelActor=ActingProcessName, DelActorCmd=ActingProcessCommandLine, DelPath=TargetFilePath;
let Rewrites = ImFileEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where EventType in~ ("FileModified","FileCreated","FileCreate")
  | where TargetFilePath has TargetDir and TargetFileName =~ "package.json"
  | where ActingProcessName has_any ("node.exe","/node")
  | project RewriteTime=TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, RewriteActor=ActingProcessName, RewriteCmd=ActingProcessCommandLine, RewriteHash=TargetFileSHA256;
Deletions
| join kind=inner Rewrites on DvcHostname
| where abs(datetime_diff('second', RewriteTime, DelTime)) <= 30
| project DelTime, RewriteTime, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, DelActor, DelActorCmd, DelPath, RewriteActor, RewriteCmd, RewriteHash
| order by DelTime desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic

    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`

| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02

    - attack.t1539

    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'

        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'

        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'

            - '\opera.exe'

    - Backup utilities reading user profile files

        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |

    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/

    category: process_creation
